where we introduced the chemical potentials

\xi=e^{2\pi iz}\ ,\qquad\bar{\xi}=e^{-2\pi i\bar{z}}\ .

(6.81)

Let us now perform the change of variable
c=2\tau_{2}s
in (
6.80
). We find

\displaystyle Z^{ST}_{con}

\displaystyle(\tau,z)=\frac{e^{\frac{4\pi z_{1}^{2}}{\tau_{2}}}\,|\theta_{1}(\tau,z)|^{2}}{\pi\sqrt{n_{5}}\,|\eta(\tau)|^{14}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}E\sum_{w,n\in\mathds{Z}}\ \sum_{n_{f}^{sl},\bar{n}_{f}^{sl}\in\mathds{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}\ \sum_{n_{f}^{su},\bar{n}_{f}^{su}\in\mathds{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\,\sum_{j_{su}=0}^{n_{5}-2}\ \sum_{l,l^{\prime}=3-n_{5}}^{n_{5}-2}\ \sum_{n_{b}^{su},\bar{n}_{b}^{su}\in\mathds{Z}}\,\mathcal{I}_{t,y}\,\mathcal{I}_{\theta}\,\mathcal{I}_{su}\,\xi^{-\frac{2}{n_{5}}\mathcal{F}}\,\bar{\xi}^{-\frac{2}{n_{5}}\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}s\,\,(q\bar{q})^{\frac{s^{2}}{n_{5}}}

\displaystyle\times\,\sum_{r,\bar{r}=0}^{\infty}\Biggl\{\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2is + r + \bar r +1 + \mathcal F + \bar{\mathcal F}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2is - r - \bar r -1 + \mathcal F + \bar{\mathcal F}}$}}\right)\left(S_{r}\,\overline{S_{\bar{r}}}-(1-S_{r})(1-\overline{S_{\bar{r}}})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\ \ +\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 1}{2is + r - \bar r + \mathcal F + \bar{\mathcal F}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2is - r + \bar r + \mathcal F + \bar{\mathcal F}}$}}\right)\left(S_{r}(1-\overline{S_{\bar{r}}})-(1-S_{r})\overline{S_{\bar{r}}})\right)\Biggr\}\delta_{\mathcal{J}_{0}-\bar{\mathcal{J}}_{0},0}\ ,

(6.82)

where we split the sums over
r,\bar{r}
over positive and negative integers, and made use of the identity (
4.68
). Here we proceed as in Section
4.3
and consider the contribution of
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathds{R})
primaries by setting
S_{r}(\tau)\to 1
. Introducing the density of states

\rho(s)=\sum_{r_{+}=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2is+r_{+}+1+\mathcal{F}+\bar{\mathcal{F}}}+\frac{1}{2is+r_{+}+1-\mathcal{F}-\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\ ,

(6.83)

this sector of the partition function reads

\displaystyle Z^{ST}_{con}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle\Big|_{\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathds{R})\text{ prim.}}=\frac{e^{\frac{4\pi z_{1}^{2}}{\tau_{2}}}\,|\theta_{1}(\tau,z)|^{2}}{\pi\sqrt{n_{5}}\,|\eta(\tau)|^{14}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}E\sum_{w,n\in\mathds{Z}}\ \sum_{n_{f}^{sl},\bar{n}_{f}^{sl}\in\mathds{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}\ \sum_{n_{f}^{su},\bar{n}_{f}^{su}\in\mathds{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\,\sum_{j_{su}=0}^{n_{5}-2}\ \sum_{l,l^{\prime}=3-n_{5}}^{n_{5}-2}\ \sum_{n_{b}^{su},\bar{n}_{b}^{su}\in\mathds{Z}}\,\mathcal{I}_{t,y}\,\mathcal{I}_{\theta}\,\mathcal{I}_{su}\,\xi^{-\frac{2}{n_{5}}\mathcal{F}}\,\bar{\xi}^{-\frac{2}{n_{5}}\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}s\,\,(q\bar{q})^{\frac{s^{2}}{n_{5}}}\,\rho(s)\ .

(6.84)

Making use of eq. (
4.76
), we can rewrite the partition function as

\displaystyle Z^{ST}_{con}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle\Big|_{\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathds{R})\text{ prim.}}=\frac{e^{\frac{4\pi z_{1}^{2}}{\tau_{2}}}\,|\theta_{1}(\tau,z)|^{2}}{\pi\sqrt{n_{5}}\,|\eta(\tau)|^{14}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}E\sum_{w,n\in\mathds{Z}}\ \sum_{n_{f}^{sl},\bar{n}_{f}^{sl}\in\mathds{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}\ \sum_{n_{f}^{su},\bar{n}_{f}^{su}\in\mathds{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\,\sum_{j_{su}=0}^{n_{5}-2}\ \sum_{l,l^{\prime}=3-n_{5}}^{n_{5}-2}\ \sum_{n_{b}^{su},\bar{n}_{b}^{su}\in\mathds{Z}}\,\mathcal{I}_{t,y}\,\mathcal{I}_{\theta}\,\mathcal{I}_{su}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}s\,\rho(s)\,\chi_{\text{c}}(s,-\mathcal{F})\overline{\chi_{\text{c}}(s,-\bar{\mathcal{F}})}\ .

(6.85)

Notice that
-\mathcal{F}
is the solution of

\mathcal{J}_{0}=\mathcal{F}+(j^{3}_{sl})_{0}=0\ ,

(6.86)

viewed as a linear equation in
(j^{3}_{sl})_{0}
. Similarly for
-\bar{\mathcal{F}}
. We can then write the characters in (
6.85
) as

\chi_{\text{c}}(s,-\mathcal{F})=\chi_{\text{c}}\left(s,(j^{3}_{sl})_{0}\right)\Biggl|_{\mathcal{J}\equiv\mathcal{F}+(j^{3}_{sl})_{0}=0}\ .

(6.87)

Reabsorbing some numerical factors into the overall normalization, we can rewrite eq. (
6.85
) as

\displaystyle Z^{ST}_{con}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle\Big|_{\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathds{R})\text{ prim.}}=\frac{e^{\frac{4\pi z_{1}^{2}}{\tau_{2}}}\,|\theta_{1}(\tau,z)|^{2}}{|\eta(\tau)|^{14}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}E\sum_{w,n\in\mathds{Z}}\ \sum_{n_{f}^{sl},\bar{n}_{f}^{sl}\in\mathds{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}\ \sum_{n_{f}^{su},\bar{n}_{f}^{su}\in\mathds{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\,\sum_{j_{su}=0}^{n_{5}-2}\ \sum_{l,l^{\prime}=3-n_{5}}^{n_{5}-2}\ \sum_{n_{b}^{su},\bar{n}_{b}^{su}\in\mathds{Z}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}\bigl((j^{3}_{sl})_{0}+(\bar{j}^{3}_{sl})_{0}\bigr)\sum_{(j^{3}_{sl})_{0}-(\bar{j}^{3}_{sl})_{0}\in\mathds{Z}}

\displaystyle\times\,\mathcal{I}_{t,y}\,\mathcal{I}_{\theta}\,\mathcal{I}_{su}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}s\,\rho(s)\,\chi_{\text{c}}(s,(j^{3}_{sl})_{0})\,\,\overline{\chi_{\text{c}}(s,(\bar{j}^{3}_{sl})_{0}})\,\delta_{\mathcal{J}_{0}-\bar{\mathcal{J}}_{0},0}\,\delta(\mathcal{J}_{0}+\bar{\mathcal{J}}_{0})\ ,

(6.88)

making manifest how the gauge constraints emerge from the path integral.

7
Discussion

In this work, we have computed the torus partition function in a wide variety of gauged Wess-Zumino-Witten models. We have shown how known results for simple cosets
G/H
can be reproduced by gauging null isometries in
G\times\tilde{H}
, where
\tilde{H}
is a copy of
H
whose action has the opposite sign. We illustrated this equivalence by computing the partition functions in both representations and demonstrating their equality for the simple cases

\frac{{SU(2)}}{U(1)}\simeq\frac{{SU(2)}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{t}}{U(1)_{L}\times U(1)_{R}}~~,~~~~\frac{SL(2,\mathds{R})}{U(1)}\simeq\frac{SL(2,\mathds{R})\times U(1)}{U(1)_{L}\times U(1)_{R}}\ .

(7.1)

The null-gauged theory also matches a standard operator approach to
G/H
coset models
Karabali:1989dk
, in which one works in the Hilbert space of
G\times\tilde{H}
, and imposes a BRST constraint that equates the excitations of
H\subset G
to those of
\tilde{H}
,
separately
among left- and right-movers. This BRST formalism is precisely that of the null-gauged model. That one arrives at the same partition function as the
G/H
coset model via this approach provides confirmation of the equivalence
\frac{G}{H}=\frac{G\times\tilde{H}}{H_{L}\times H_{R}}
.

We furthermore demonstrated the equivalence of the partition functions for the cosets
18
18

18

The null-gauged formulation was discussed in
Israel:2004ir
, but the partition function was evaluated only in the coset orbifold description.

\bigg(\frac{SL(2,\mathds{R})_{n_{5}}}{U(1)}\times\frac{{SU(2)}_{n_{5}}}{U(1)}\bigg)\Big/{\mathbb{Z}}_{n_{5}}\simeq\frac{SL(2,\mathds{R})_{n_{5}}\times{SU(2)}_{n_{5}}}{U(1)_{L}\times U(1)_{R}}\ ,

(7.2)

describing
n_{5}
NS5-branes distributed in a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{n_{5}}
-symmetric fashion in a transverse plane.

Having established the equivalence of the null-gauged models to conventional coset CFT’s, we evaluated the partition function of null-gauged models for the supertube geometries (
1.5
), for which there is no alternative construction. The fact that we were able to establish the equivalence to standard cosets in the warm-up exercises and the decomposition into characters we carried out gives us confidence that eq. (
6.2.3
) is the right result. Note that in arriving at this result, we have made no specialization to any particular supertube background; it applies to any of them, even the non-supersymmetric JMaRT solution. It was argued in
Martinec:2018nco
that the fivebrane decoupling limit of JMaRT is stable at the level of perturbative string theory; here we see confirmation of this result, in that there are no pathologies in the one-loop partition function.

While we have focused on the computation of the partition function, our methods are closely related to the computation of the elliptic genus, which has for instance been computed for coset orbifold constructions of fivebrane backgrounds in
Eguchi:2003yy
;
Eguchi:2004ik
;
Eguchi:2004yi
;
Giveon:2015raa
. The elliptic genus provides a topological invariant of a given string background, in which the right-movers are in a (worldsheet) supersymmetric ground state while the left-movers are arbitrarily excited. These states embed in string theory as BPS states once we compactify an additional direction on a circle
Harvey:2013mda
, see also
Giveon:2015raa
. String states with momentum
p
and winding
w
on
{\mathbb{S}}^{1}
must satisfy the Virasoro level-matching constraint

N_{L}-N_{R}=pw~;

(7.3)

BPS perturbative string states having
N_{R}=0
then have some nonzero
N_{L}
that is counted by the elliptic genus of the worldsheet CFT.

This embedding is not quite available for the supertube. A candidate for the circle would be
{\mathbb{S}}^{1}_{y}
, except it is not entirely physical since the gauge orbits are partly along
y
. Nevertheless we can identify the analogous states here as those which have nonzero
n_{y}w_{y}
while also having no right-moving oscillator excitation beyond that needed to compensate the Casimir energy (
-\frac{1}{2}
in the NS sector,
0
in the R sector). Then
n_{y}w_{y}
forces left-moving oscillator excitation, and the partition function in this sector plays the role of the elliptic genus of the supertube. As in the coset orbifold that appears in double-scaled little string theory
Giveon:1999px
;
Giveon:1999tq
, there are contributions to this quantity from both the discrete series and from the bottom of the long string continuum, if one follows the same manipulations performed there
Eguchi:2010cb
;
Giveon:2015raa
. One also finds an analogous appearance of mock-modularity in the partition sums involved.

Our supertube partition functions mirror a similar analysis of perturbative strings in the Euclidean BTZ black hole background
Maldacena:2000kv
. More recently, this partition function has been revisited in
Ashok:2022vdz
(see also
Ashok:2020dnc
) with an eye toward extracting a stringy quasinormal mode spectrum. It would be interesting to see whether a similar analysis can be performed for the supertube backgrounds, and whether such modes might be related to the absorption of winding strings into the background.

Also of interest would be to investigate torus correlation functions. The two-point function of light string vertex operators on the torus computes the OPE coefficients for the emission/absorption of such excitations onto a highly excited string (if we look at the appropriate limit of the amplitude). It would be interesting to compare such an amplitude to the absorption/emission amplitude onto the long string inside the fivebrane that describes BPS black hole microstates
Callan:1996dv
;
Das:1996wn
;
Maldacena:1996ix
, in particular for supertubes with a deep throat with large redshift to the cap of the geometry.

Finally, the supertube cosets (
1.5
) have a modulus
R_{y}
that descends from a marginal deformation of the parent WZW model.
19
19

19

Naively, the circular array of fivebranes (
1.2
) also has a modulus, namely the radius of the circle, which controls the value of the dilaton at the tip of the
\frac{SL(2,\mathds{R})}{U(1)}
“cigar”. However, in the worldsheet theory this parameter is a scaling parameter rather than a modulus per se. One way to see this feature is through the quantum equivalence of this coset CFT to the
\mathcal{N}=2
Liouville CFT
Giveon:1999px
;
Giveon:1999tq
, in which the corresponding parameter is the coefficient of the exponential Liouville superpotential. This parameter can be shifted away by a field redefinition, whose effect is a (KPZ) scaling relation among correlation functions
Knizhnik:1988ak
;
David:1988hj
;
Distler:1988jt
of a single theory, rather than a moduli space of CFT’s. While one might call this parameter a modulus, it is a rather trivial one, in that the spectrum of the theory does not depend on it, and the OPE coefficients only depend on it via an overall coefficient.

The moduli of WZW models consist of current-current deformations
\mathcal{C}_{ab}J^{a}\bar{J}^{b}
; as long as such a deformation is invariant under the gauge group
H
, it leads to a moduli space of the coset theory.
Also, any continuous parameter(s) specifying inequivalent embeddings of the gauge group results in a moduli space of CFT’s, see for example
Giveon:1993ph
. Since, as we have seen, null gauging provides a general framework for describing such CFT’s, the methods developed above can be extended to a computation of their partition functions.

For instance, the deformation of
AdS_{3}
to an asymptotically linear dilaton background
20
20

20

Often mischaracterized as a “single-trace
T\bar{T}
deformation” of the dual CFT – this property only holds at the symmetric orbifold locus in the CFT moduli space.

has been recast as an example of null gauging
Chakraborty:2019mdf
.
The computation of the worldsheet partition function of these backgrounds, exactly as a function of the deformation parameter, should be a minor modification of the computations described in Sections
3
and
4
.
21
21

21

See
Israel:2003ry
and
Chakraborty:2024mls
for alternative approaches to this computation.

Appendix A
Theta functions

In this appendix we spell out our conventions for Theta functions and collect various properties they obey. We define

\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{j}(\tau,z)=\sum_{n\in\mathds{Z}}q^{\kappa(n+\frac{j}{2\kappa})^{2}}\xi^{k(n+\frac{j}{2\kappa})}\ ,\qquad q=e^{2\pi i\tau}\ ,\quad\xi=e^{2\pi iz}\ ,

(A.1)

and

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle=i\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}q^{\frac{1}{2}(n-\frac{1}{2})^{2}}\xi^{n-\frac{1}{2}}=2\sin(\pi z)\,q^{\frac{1}{12}}\,\eta(\tau)\,\prod_{m=1}^{\infty}(1-\xi q^{m})(1-\xi^{-1}q^{m})\ ,

(A.2a)

\displaystyle\theta_{2}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}q^{\frac{1}{2}(n-\frac{1}{2})^{2}}\xi^{n-\frac{1}{2}}=2\cos(\pi z)\,q^{\frac{1}{12}}\,\eta(\tau)\,\prod_{m=1}^{\infty}(1+\xi q^{m})(1+\xi^{-1}q^{m})\ ,

(A.2b)

\displaystyle\theta_{3}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}q^{\frac{n^{2}}{2}}\xi^{n}=q^{-\frac{1}{24}}\,\eta(\tau)\,\prod_{m=1}^{\infty}(1+\xi q^{m-\frac{1}{2}})(1+\xi^{-1}q^{m-\frac{1}{2}})\ ,

(A.2c)

\displaystyle\theta_{4}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}q^{\frac{n^{2}}{2}}\xi^{n}=q^{-\frac{1}{24}}\,\eta(\tau)\,\prod_{m=1}^{\infty}(1-\xi q^{m-\frac{1}{2}})(1-\xi^{-1}q^{m-\frac{1}{2}})\ ,

(A.2d)

where

\eta(\tau)=q^{1/24}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{n})\ .

(A.3)

Let us now list a number of relation among Theta functions, which will be used in the main text, see
e.g.

Eguchi:2003yy
;
Eguchi:2010cb
:

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle=\Theta^{(2)}_{1}(\tau,z)-\Theta^{(2)}_{-1}(\tau,z)\ ,

(A.4)

\displaystyle\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{\ell}(\tau,-z)

\displaystyle=\Theta_{-\ell}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z)\ ,

(A.5)

\displaystyle\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{\ell+2\kappa}(\tau,z)

\displaystyle=\Theta_{\ell}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z)\ ,

(A.6)

\displaystyle\Theta_{\ell}^{(\kappa)}\left(\tau,z+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1}{\kappa}$}}\tau\right)

\displaystyle=q^{-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{4\kappa}}\,e^{-\pi im_{1}z}\,\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{\ell+m_{1}}(\tau,z)\ ,\qquad\forall\,m_{1}\in\mathds{Z}\ ,

(A.7)

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m_{1}+m_{2}}q^{-\frac{m_{1}^{2}}{2}}e^{-2\pi im_{1}z}\theta_{1}(\tau,z)\ ,\quad\forall\,m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathds{Z}\ ,

(A.8)

\displaystyle\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{\ell}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})

\displaystyle=q^{-\frac{km_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-\pi i\kappa m_{1}z}\,e^{\pi i\ell m_{2}}\,\Theta_{\ell+m_{1}\kappa}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z)\ ,\qquad\forall\,m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathds{Z}\ ,

(A.9)

In Section
3
we will also need the following identity,
22
22

22

See
Elitzur:1989nr
;
Chang2019
for similar derivations.

\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{2}\,e^{-\frac{\pi\kappa}{\tau_{2}}u_{2}^{2}}\,\Theta_{\ell}^{(k)}(\tau,u)\overline{\Theta_{\ell^{\prime}}^{(k)}(\tau,u)}=\,\delta_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\sum_{n\in\mathds{Z}}\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{1}\,e^{-\pi\,\kappa\,\tau_{2}\left(s_{1}+2(n+\frac{\ell}{2\kappa})\right)^{2}}\ ,

(A.10)

where
\ell,\ell^{\prime}=-\kappa+1,\dots,\kappa
and
u=s_{1}\tau+s_{2}
. Let us derive it. From the definitions (
A.1
) and (
2.42
) we have

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{\pi\kappa}{\tau_{2}}u_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\,\Theta_{\ell}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,u)\overline{\Theta_{\ell^{\prime}}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,u)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n,n^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}}{\rm exp}\Bigl[-\pi\kappa\Bigl(\tau_{2}\,s_{1}^{2}+2\tau_{2}(n+\tfrac{\ell}{2\kappa})^{2}+2\tau_{2}(n^{\prime}+\tfrac{\ell^{\prime}}{2\kappa})^{2}\Bigr)+2\pi i\kappa\tau_{1}\Bigl((n+\tfrac{\ell}{2\kappa})^{2}-(n^{\prime}+\tfrac{\ell^{\prime}}{2\kappa})^{2}\Bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt\times\,{\rm exp}\Bigl[-2\pi\kappa s_{1}\tau_{2}\left(n+\tfrac{\ell}{2\kappa}+n^{\prime}+\tfrac{\ell^{\prime}}{2\kappa}\right)+2\pi i\kappa s_{1}\tau_{1}\left(n+\tfrac{\ell}{2\kappa}-n^{\prime}-\tfrac{\ell^{\prime}}{2\kappa}\right)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt\times\,{\rm exp}\Bigl[2\pi i\kappa s_{2}\Bigl((n+\tfrac{\ell}{2\kappa})-(n^{\prime}+\tfrac{\ell^{\prime}}{2\kappa})\Bigr)\Bigr]\ .

(A.11)

Using the Kronecker delta representation

\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\pi ixs}\text{d}s=\delta_{x,0}\ ,

(A.12)

we can evaluate the integral over
s_{2}
and the last line in (
A.11
) gives

\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{2}\,{\rm exp}\Bigl[2\pi i\kappa s_{2}\Bigl((n+\tfrac{\ell}{2\kappa})-(n^{\prime}+\tfrac{\ell^{\prime}}{2\kappa})\Bigr)\Bigr]=\delta_{\kappa(n-n^{\prime})+\frac{\ell-\ell^{\prime}}{2},0}=\delta_{n,n^{\prime}}\,\delta_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\ ,

(A.13)

where the second equality follows from
-\kappa+1\leq\ell,\ell^{\prime}\leq\kappa
. Enforcing the delta functions and completing the square one obtains eq. (
A.10
) as claimed.

Appendix B
Character formulae of the
\bm{SU(2)}

WZW model and related coset theories

Let us review some character formulae for the
SU(2)
WZW model, the parafermion coset and the
\mathcal{N}=2
minimal model
\frac{SU(2)}{U(1)}
.

Bosonic
\mathfrak{su}\bm{(2)_{\kappa-2}}
WZW model

The characters of the bosonic
SU(2)
WZW model at level
\kappa-2
read
DiFrancesco:1997nk

\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,j}(\tau,z_{B})=\frac{\Theta_{j+1}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z_{B})-\Theta_{-j-1}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z_{B})}{\Theta_{1}^{(2)}(\tau,z_{B})-\Theta_{-1}^{(2)}(\tau,z_{B})}\ ,\qquad j=0\,,\dots\,,\kappa-2\ ,

(B.1)

where Theta functions have been defined in eq. (
A.1
), the suffix
B
stands for bosonic, as opposed to the supersymmetric
SU(2)
WZW theory and
z_{B}
is the chemical potential associated to the
SU(2)
Cartan
j_{0}^{3}
(similarly for the anti-holomoprhic sector). The diagonal modular invariant partition function is

\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,z_{B})=\text{Tr}\left[q^{L_{0}-\frac{1}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{1}{24}}e^{2\pi iz_{B}\,j_{0}^{3}}e^{-2\pi i\bar{z}_{B}\,\bar{j}_{0}^{3}}\right]=\sum_{j=0}^{\kappa-2}\left|\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,j}(\tau,z_{B})\right|^{2}\ .

(B.2)

Let us study some periodicity properties of the characters (
B.1
), which are used in the main text. Consider the denominator in eq. (
B.1
). It follows from (
A.4
) and (
A.8
) that for any
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathds{Z}
,

\Theta_{1}^{(2)}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})-\Theta_{-1}^{(2)}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})\\
=(-1)^{m_{1}+m_{2}}\,q^{-\frac{m_{1}^{2}}{2}}\,e^{-2\pi im_{1}z}\,\left(\Theta_{1}^{(2)}(\tau,z)-\Theta_{-1}^{(2)}(\tau,z)\right)\ .

(B.3)

Let us now consider the numerator of (
B.1
). We first consider
m_{1}\in 2\mathds{Z}
. From (
A.6
) and (
A.9
) it follows

\displaystyle\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{j+1}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(j+1)m_{2}}q^{-\frac{\kappa m_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-\pi i\kappa m_{1}z}\,\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{j+1}(\tau,z)\ ,

(B.4)

\displaystyle\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{-j-1}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(j+1)m_{2}}q^{-\frac{\kappa m_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-\pi i\kappa m_{1}z}\,\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{-j-1}(\tau,z)

(B.5)

and hence

\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,j}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})=(-1)^{jm_{2}}\,q^{-\frac{(\kappa-2)m_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-\pi i(\kappa-2)m_{1}z}\,\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,j}(\tau,z)\ .

(B.6)

Let us now consider
m_{1}\in 2\mathds{Z}+1
. Using again (
A.6
) and (
A.9
) we find

\Theta_{j+1}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})=(-1)^{(j+1)m_{2}}q^{-\frac{\kappa m_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-\pi i\kappa m_{1}z}\,\Theta_{-1-(\kappa-2-j)}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z)

(B.7)

and similarly

\Theta_{-j-1}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})=(-1)^{(j+1)m_{2}}q^{-\frac{\kappa m_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-\pi i\kappa m_{1}z}\,\Theta_{1+(\kappa-2-j)}^{(\kappa)}(\tau,z)\ .

(B.8)

It follows that

\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,j}(\tau,z_{B}+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})=(-1)^{(j+1)m_{2}}\,q^{-\frac{(\kappa-2)m_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-\pi i(\kappa-2)m_{1}z_{B}}\,\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,\kappa-2-j}(\tau,z_{B})\ .

(B.9)

The parafermion theory

The
\mathfrak{su}(2)_{\kappa-2}
characters (
B.1
) can be decomposed as

\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,j}(\tau,z_{B})=\sum_{\ell=3-\kappa}^{\kappa-2}\chi_{B,j,\ell}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)\,\frac{\Theta_{\ell}^{(\kappa-2)}(\tau,z_{B})}{\eta(\tau)}\ ,

(B.10)

where
\eta(\tau)
is the Dedekind eta function (
A.3
) and we introduced the characters
\chi_{B,j,\ell}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)
of the parafermion coset
\frac{SU(2)_{\kappa-2}}{U(1)}
.
The parafermion characters vanish for
j+\ell\in 2\mathds{Z}+1

DiFrancesco:1997nk
,

\displaystyle\chi_{B,j,\ell}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)

\displaystyle=0\ ,\qquad j+\ell\text{ odd}\ ,

(B.11)

and obey the identifications
DiFrancesco:1997nk

\chi_{B,\,j,\ell}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)=\chi_{B,\,j,-\ell}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)=\chi_{B,\,j,\ell+2k_{B}}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)=\chi_{B,\,k_{B}-j,\ell+k_{B}}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)\ .

(B.12)

The diagonal invariant partition function for the parafermion coset is
Gepner:1986hr

\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\kappa-2}\sum_{\ell=3-\kappa}^{\kappa-2}\Bigl|\chi_{B,\,j,\ell}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)\Bigr|^{2}\ ,

(B.13)

where the factor of
\frac{1}{2}
is due to the identification
\chi_{B,\,j,\ell}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)=\chi_{B,\,k-j,\ell+\kappa}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau)
.

The
\bm{\mathcal{N}=2}
minimal model

The characters
\chi^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}_{S,\,j,\ell,s}(\tau,z)
of the
\mathcal{N}=2
minimal model
\frac{SU(2)}{U(1)}
are defined by
Gepner:1987qi
;
Eguchi:2004ik
,

\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,j}(\tau,z_{B})\,\Theta_{s}^{(2)}(\tau,z_{F})=\sum_{\ell=-\kappa+1}^{\kappa}\chi^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}_{S,\,j,\ell,s}(\tau,z)\,\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{\ell}(\tau,u)\ ,\qquad s=-1,0,1,2\ ,

(B.14)

where the superscript S stands for
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetric, as opposed to the bosonic parafermion theory. Let us remind that in (
B.14
) the chemical potentials are related by eq. (
3.6
). The characters vanish for
Gepner:1987qi

\chi^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}_{S,j,\ell,s}(\tau,z)=0\ ,\qquad j+\ell+s\in 2\mathds{Z}+1\ ,

(B.15)

and obey the identification
Gepner:1987qi
;
Eguchi:2004ik

\chi^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}_{S,\,\kappa-j-2,\,\ell+\kappa,\,s+2}(\tau,z)=\chi^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}_{S,\,j,\ell,s}(\tau,z)

(B.16)

From eq. (
B.14
) for
s=-1
and
s=1
and eq. (
A.4
), it follows in particular

\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{B,j}(\tau,z_{B})\,\theta_{1}(\tau,z_{F})=\sum_{\ell=-\kappa+1}^{\kappa}I_{\ell}^{j}(\tau,z)\Theta^{(\kappa)}_{\ell}(\tau,u)\ ,

(B.17)

where

I^{j}_{\ell}(\tau,z)=\chi^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}_{S,\,j,\ell,1}(\tau,z)-\chi^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}_{S,\,j,\ell,-1}(\tau,z)

(B.18)

are the
\mathcal{N}=2
coset characters in the
\tilde{\text{R}}
sector. It follows from eq. (
B.16
) that they obey

I^{j}_{\ell}(\tau,z)=-I^{\kappa-j-2}_{\ell+\kappa}(\tau,z)=I^{j}_{\ell-2\kappa}(\tau,z)\ .

(B.19)

From eqs. (
B.15
) and (
B.18
) we also find that

I^{j}_{\ell}(\tau,z)=0\ ,\qquad\text{for}\quad j+\ell\in 2\mathds{Z}\ .

(B.20)

The diagonal modular invariant partition function of the
\mathcal{N}=2
minimal model in the
\tilde{\text{R}}
sector reads
Gepner:1986hr
;
Cappelli:1986hf

\mathcal{Z}_{S}^{\frac{\mathfrak{su}(2)}{\mathfrak{u}(1)}}(\tau,z)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\kappa-2}\sum_{\ell=-\kappa+1}^{\kappa}I^{j}_{\ell}(\tau,z)\overline{I^{j}_{\ell}(\tau,z)}\ .

(B.21)

Appendix C
The
\bm{U(1)}
partition function

In this appendix we discuss partition functions of free bosons for symmetric and asymmetric gauging.

C.1
Symmetric gauging

Consider a compact boson of radius
R
on the torus, with boundary conditions

\displaystyle X(\sigma_{1}+2\pi,\sigma_{2})

\displaystyle=X(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})+2\pi R\,m_{1}+2\pi s_{1}\ ,

(C.1a)

\displaystyle X(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}+2\pi)

\displaystyle=X(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})-2\pi R\,m_{2}-2\pi s_{2}\ ,

(C.1b)

where
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in[0,2\pi]
parametrize the torus and
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathds{Z}
. Let us compute the partition function from both the operator formalism and the path integral and see that the two results agree.

Path integral formalism

Let us recall that for
s_{1}=s_{2}=0
, the partition function

Z(\tau,u=0)=\int\mathcal{D}X\,e^{-S}\ ,\qquad\text{with}\qquad S=\frac{1}{\pi}\int\text{d}^{2}v\,\partial_{v}X\partial_{\bar{v}}X\ ,

(C.2)

can be computed in the path integral formalism and reads
DiFrancesco:1997nk

Z(\tau,u=0)=\frac{1}{|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{w,p\in\mathds{Z}}q^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}+Rw\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}-Rw\right)^{2}}\ .

(C.3)

Let us now consider the derivation for generic
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
. In order to enforce
both
the boundary conditions (
C.1
), we introduce the free boson

X^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})=X(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})-\Phi(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\ ,\qquad\text{with}\quad\Phi(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})=-\sigma_{1}s_{1}+\sigma_{2}s_{2}\ .

(C.4)

We can then repeat the derivation of
DiFrancesco:1997nk
with the free boson
X^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
in place of
X(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
. The action is now

S=\frac{1}{\pi}\int\text{d}^{2}v\,\partial_{v}X^{\prime}\partial_{\bar{v}}X^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int\text{d}^{2}v\,\partial_{v}(X-\Phi)\partial_{\bar{v}}(X-\Phi)\ ,

(C.5)

and one obtains

Z(\tau,\bar{\tau},u,\bar{u})=\frac{R}{\sqrt{\tau_{2}}\,|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathds{Z}}{\rm exp}\left(-\frac{\pi R^{2}}{\tau_{2}}\left|m_{1}\tau+m_{2}+\frac{u}{R}\right|^{2}\right)\ .

(C.6)

We remind the reader that
u
is related to
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
by eq. (
2.42
). Poisson resumming eq. (
C.6
) we find

Z(\tau,u)=\frac{e^{-\frac{\pi}{\tau_{2}}u_{2}^{2}}}{|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{w,p\in\mathds{Z}}q^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}+wR\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}-wR\right)^{2}}\,e^{\pi iu(\frac{p}{R}+wR)}\,e^{\pi i\bar{u}(\frac{p}{R}-wR)}\ .

(C.7)

Since

u_{2}=s_{1}\tau_{2}\ ,

(C.8)

the partition function can equivalently be written as

Z(\tau,u)=\frac{q^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}\bar{q}^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}}{|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{w,\,p\,\in\,\mathds{Z}}q^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}+wR\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}-wR\right)^{2}}\,e^{\pi iu(\frac{p}{R}+Rw)}\,e^{\pi i\bar{u}(\frac{p}{R}-wR)}\ .

(C.9)

Operator formalism

In the operator formalism we compute the partition function as a trace over the Hilbert space. Let us start by decomposing the boson
X
into holomorphic and anti-holomorphic contributions,

X=X_{L}(v)+X_{R}(\bar{v})\ ,\qquad\partial_{\bar{v}}X_{L}(v)=0\ ,\qquad\partial_{v}X_{R}(\bar{v})=0\ .

(C.10)

Since
\partial_{v}X_{L}(v)
and
\partial_{\bar{v}}X_{R}(\bar{v})

are periodic, we obtain the Fourier series,

\begin{split}X_{L}(v)&=a_{L}+b_{L}v-i\sum_{n\neq 0}\frac{\alpha_{n}}{n}e^{inv}\ ,\\
X_{R}(\bar{v})&=a_{R}+b_{R}\bar{v}-i\sum_{n\neq 0}\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{n}}{n}e^{in\bar{v}}\ .\end{split}

(C.11)

Enforcing the boundary condition (
C.1a
) we find,

b_{L}+b_{R}=m_{1}R+s_{1}\ ,

(C.12)

that we solve as

b_{L}=\frac{p_{L}+s_{1}}{2}\ ,\qquad b_{R}=\frac{-p_{R}+s_{1}}{2}\ ,\qquad p_{L}-p_{R}=2m_{1}R\ .

(C.13)

In the operator formalism eq. (
C.1b
) is implemented as a twist in the trace partition function. The partition sum we wish to compute is then

\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}}[gq^{H}\bar{g}\bar{q}^{\bar{H}}]=\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}[gq^{H}]\,\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}[\bar{g}\bar{q}^{\bar{H}}]\ ,

(C.14)

where the twists read

g=e^{\pi i(p_{L}+\alpha s_{1})s_{2}}\ ,\qquad\bar{g}=e^{\pi i(p_{R}-\alpha s_{1})s_{2}}\ ,

(C.15)

for some
\alpha\in\mathds{R}
. The precise value of
\alpha
in (
C.15
) does not matter here, since it cancels when assembling holomorphic and anti-holomorphic sectors.
The Hamiltonian for the holomorphic sector is

H=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\text{d}\sigma_{1}\,\partial_{v}X(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}=0)\,\partial_{v}X(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}=0)-\frac{1}{24}=b_{L}^{2}-\frac{1}{24}+\sum_{n>0}\alpha_{n}\alpha_{-n}\ ,

(C.16)

and similarly for the anti-holomorphic sector. The holomorphic contribution to the partition function thus reads

Tr_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}[gq^{H}]=\frac{1}{\eta(\tau)}\,\sum_{p_{L}}q^{\frac{(p_{L}+s_{1})^{2}}{4}}e^{\pi ip_{L}s_{2}}=q^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}\sum_{p_{L}}q^{\frac{p_{L}^{2}}{4}}e^{\pi ip_{L}u}\ .

(C.17)

Putting together holomorphic and anti-holomorphic sectors,

Z(\tau,\bar{\tau},u,\bar{u})=Tr_{\mathcal{H}}[gq^{H}\bar{g}\bar{q}^{\bar{H}}]=\frac{q^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}\bar{q}^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}}{|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{p_{L},p_{R}}q^{\frac{p_{L}^{2}}{4}}\,\bar{q}^{\frac{p_{R}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{\pi ip_{L}u}\,e^{\pi ip_{R}\bar{u}}\ .

(C.18)

Let us specify the lattice of the momenta. From eq. (
C.13
) it follows

p_{L}-p_{R}\in 2R\,\mathds{Z}\ .

(C.19)

Furthermore, we have

Z(\tau,\bar{\tau},u,\bar{u})=Z(\tau+1,\bar{\tau}+1,u,\bar{u})=e^{\frac{\pi i(p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2})}{2}}\,Z(\tau,\bar{\tau},u,\bar{u})\ ,

(C.20)

and hence modular invariance implies

(p_{L}-p_{R})(p_{L}+p_{R})\in 4\mathds{Z}\ .

(C.21)

Using (
C.19
) we find

p_{L}+p_{R}\in\frac{2}{R}\mathds{Z}\ .

(C.22)

We thus find that the momenta obey the usual quantization,

p_{L}=\frac{n}{R}+wR\ ,\qquad p_{R}=\frac{n}{R}-wR\ ,

(C.23)

and the partition function reads

Z(\tau,u)=\frac{q^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}\bar{q}^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}}{|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{w,\,p\,\in\,\mathds{Z}}q^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}+wR\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}-wR\right)^{2}}\,e^{\pi iu(\frac{p}{R}+Rw)}\,e^{\pi i\bar{u}(\frac{p}{R}-Rw)}\ ,

(C.24)

which agrees with eq. (
C.9
). As we review in Appendix
D
, in the operator formalism the partition function is often defined omitting the prefactor
q^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}\bar{q}^{\frac{s_{1}^{2}}{4}}=e^{-\frac{\pi}{\tau_{2}}u_{2}^{2}}
in (
C.24
). In fact, the free compact boson partition function is often defined as

\mathcal{Z}(\tau,u)=\frac{1}{|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{w,\,p\,\in\,\mathds{Z}}q^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}+wR\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}-wR\right)^{2}}\,e^{\pi iu(\frac{p}{R}+Rw)}\,e^{\pi i\bar{u}(\frac{p}{R}-Rw)}\ .

(C.25)

This results in different modular properties.

C.2
Asymmetric gauging

Let us now consider a slight variation of eq. (
C.5
) and compute

Z(\tau,u)=\int\mathcal{D}Xe^{-S_{1}-S_{2}}\ ,

(C.26)

with

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int\text{d}^{2}v\,\partial_{v}(X-r\Phi)\,\partial_{\bar{v}}(X-\ell\Phi)\ ,

(C.27)

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\ell-r)^{2}}{4\pi}\int\text{d}^{2}v\,\partial_{v}\Phi\,\partial_{\bar{v}}\Phi\ .

and
\ell,r\in\mathds{R}
. Notice that for
\ell=r
, eq. (
C.26
) reduces to (
C.5
). We will also see that the same partition function can be computed in the operator formalism and that the two results agree.

Path integral formalism

The contribution of
S_{2}
is easy to compute,

\displaystyle S_{2}=\frac{(\ell-r)^{2}}{4\pi}\int\text{d}^{2}v\,\left(\frac{-i\bar{u}}{2\tau_{2}}\right)\left(\frac{iu}{2\tau_{2}}\right)=\frac{\pi\,(\ell-r)^{2}\,|u|^{2}}{4\,\tau_{2}}\ .

(C.28)

Since

\partial_{v}\Phi=-\frac{i\bar{u}}{2\tau_{2}}\ ,\qquad\partial_{\bar{v}}\Phi=\frac{iu}{2\tau_{2}}\ ,

(C.29)

the contribution of
S_{1}
simply follows from the discussion of Appendix
C.1
with the replacements

u\mapsto\ell u\ ,\qquad\bar{u}\mapsto r\bar{u}\ .

(C.30)

We obtain

Z(\tau,u)=\frac{R\,e^{-\frac{\pi\,(\ell-r)^{2}\,|u|^{2}}{4\,\tau_{2}}}}{\sqrt{\tau_{2}}\,|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathds{Z}}{\rm exp}\Bigl(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\pi R^2}{\tau_2}$}}\left(m_{1}\tau+m_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\ell\, u}{R}$}}\right)\left(m_{1}\bar{\tau}+m_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r \, \bar u}{R}$}}\right)\Bigr)\ .

(C.31)

Operator formalism

Since in the operator formalism holomorphic and anti-holomorphic characters are first independently computed and then assembled to define the partition function, a natural notion of asymmetric gauging is mapping
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
to

s_{1}\mapsto\ell s_{1}\ ,\qquad s_{2}\mapsto\ell s_{2}\ ,

(C.32)

in the holomorphic sector and

s_{1}\mapsto rs_{1}\ ,\qquad s_{2}\mapsto rs_{2}\ ,

(C.33)

in the anti-holomorphic sector. This notion of gauging is also the one adopted in asymmetric orbifolds, see
e.g.

Aoki:2004sm
. Eq. (
C.24
) is replaced by

Z(\tau,u)=\frac{e^{\pi i\alpha(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}}}{|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\,e^{\frac{\pi iu_{2}^{2}}{2\tau_{2}^{2}}(\ell^{2}\tau-r^{2}\bar{\tau})}\sum_{w,p\in\mathds{Z}}q^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}+wR\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}-wR\right)^{2}}\,e^{\pi i\ell(\frac{p}{R}+Rw)u}\,e^{\pi ir(\frac{p}{R}-Rw)\bar{u}}\ ,

(C.34)

where the extra factor
e^{\pi i\alpha(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}}
is due to the phases in (
C.15
), no longer cancelling against each other. Poisson resumming eq. (
C.34
) we obtain

Z(\tau,u)=\frac{R}{\sqrt{\tau_{2}}\,|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\,e^{\pi i\alpha(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}}\,e^{\frac{\pi iu_{2}^{2}}{2\tau_{2}^{2}}(\ell^{2}\tau-r^{2}\bar{\tau})}\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{4\tau_{2}}(\ell u-r\bar{u})^{2}}\\
\sum_{w,m\in\mathds{Z}}{\rm exp}\Bigl(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\pi R^2}{\tau_2}$}}\left(m+w\tau+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\ell u}{R}$}}\right)\left(m+w\bar{\tau}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r \bar u}{R}$}}\right)\Bigr)\ .

(C.35)

Using eq. (
2.42
) it is not difficult to check that for

\alpha=\frac{1}{2}\ ,

(C.36)

one has

\pi i\alpha(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}+\frac{\pi iu_{2}^{2}}{2\tau_{2}^{2}}(\ell^{2}\tau-r^{2}\bar{\tau})-\frac{\pi}{4\tau_{2}}(\ell u-r\bar{u})^{2}=-\frac{\pi(\ell-r)^{2}|u|^{2}}{4\tau_{2}}\ ,

(C.37)

and hence eqs. (
C.31
) and (
C.35
) agree.

Free-fermion radius

At the free-fermion radius
R=\sqrt{2}
(in our conventions
\alpha^{\prime}=1
) we have
Ginsparg:1988ui

\sum_{w,p\in\mathds{Z}}q^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}+wR\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{p}{R}-wR\right)^{2}}\,e^{\pi i\ell(\frac{p}{R}+wR)u}\,e^{\pi ir(\frac{p}{R}-wR)\bar{u}}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\theta_{i}(\tau,\tfrac{\ell}{\sqrt{2}}u)\overline{\theta_{i}(\tau,\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}}u)}\ ,

(C.38)

and the partition function can be rewritten as

Z(\tau,u)=\frac{e^{\frac{\pi i}{2}(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}}}{2|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\,e^{\frac{\pi iu_{2}^{2}}{2\tau_{2}^{2}}(\ell^{2}\tau-r^{2}\bar{\tau})}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\theta_{i}(\tau,\tfrac{\ell}{\sqrt{2}}u)\overline{\theta_{i}(\tau,\tfrac{r}{\sqrt{2}}u)}\ .

(C.39)

Appendix D
Different frames for the partition function

There are two different notions of “partition function” in 2D CFT
Kraus:2006nb
. One, which we will refer to as
trace
partition function, is the character formula

\mathcal{Z}(\tau,u)=\text{Tr}\left[q^{L_{0}-\frac{1}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{1}{24}}e^{2\pi iuJ}e^{-2\pi i\bar{u}\bar{J}}\right]\ ,

(D.1)

where
u
(respectively
\bar{u}
) is the chemical potential associated to the current
J
(respectively
\bar{J}
). In the examples discussed in the main text,
J
will be identified with either the
\mathcal{R}
-charge or the momentum of some free boson. The trace partition function tipically obeys a modular transformation of the schematic form

\mathcal{Z}\left(-\frac{1}{\tau},\frac{u}{\tau}\right)=e^{\frac{\pi i\,\hat{c}\,u^{2}}{\tau}}e^{-\frac{\pi i\,\hat{c}\,\bar{u}^{2}}{\bar{\tau}}}\,\mathcal{Z}(\tau,u)\ ,

(D.2)

for some
\hat{c}\in\mathds{R}
. The second notion of “partition function”, which we are going to call
path integral
partition function, is given by

Z(\tau,u)=\int\mathcal{D}g\,e^{-S}\ ,

(D.3)

where
S
is the action of the model under consideration, coupled to background gauge fields for the current
J
. In the path integral, a modular transformation is implemented as a change of coordinates followed by a Weyl transformation and hence the path integral partition function is automatically modular invariant,

Z\Big(-\frac{1}{\tau},\frac{u}{\tau}\,\Big)=Z(\tau,u)\ .

(D.4)

To avoid confusion, in the following as well as in the main text, we denote path integral partition functions by
Z
and reserve the curly character
\mathcal{Z}
for trace partition functions.
Given the different modular transformations, it is clear that these two notions of partition function do not coincide exactly. They are typically related as
Kraus:2006nb

Z(\tau,u,\bar{u})=e^{\frac{2\pi\hat{c}}{\tau_{2}}u_{1}^{2}}\mathcal{Z}(\tau,u,\bar{u})\ ,

(D.5)

where, for example,

\hat{c}=\frac{1}{2}

(D.6)

for a compact boson and we saw in Section
3
that for the
\mathcal{N}=2
minimal model defined by the coset
\frac{SU(2)_{\kappa}}{U(1)}
we have

\hat{c}=\frac{\kappa-2}{\kappa}\ .

(D.7)

In fact, one can check that the factor
e^{\frac{2\pi\hat{c}}{\tau_{2}}u_{1}^{2}}
in eq. (
D.5
) offsets the exponential factors in (
D.2
).

Relating path integral and trace partition functions for the
\bm{SU(2)}
WZW model

In this section we derive some relations between trace and path integral partition functions for the
SU(2)
WZW model. The discussion is not entirely trivial since, to the best of our knowledge, we are not aware of a path integral derivation of the partition function of the
SU(2)
WZW model.

Symmetric embeddings

We define the path integral partition function as

Z_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,z_{B})=\int\mathcal{D}g_{su}\,{\rm exp}\left(-(\kappa-2)S_{su}^{B}(g_{su},a[z_{B}],a[z_{B}]^{\dagger})\right)\ ,

(D.8)

see eq. (
2.50
), with embeddings

\varepsilon_{L}^{su}=\varepsilon_{R}^{su}\equiv\varepsilon=-\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}\ ,

(D.9)

The trace partition function of the bosonic
SU(2)_{\kappa-2}
model,

\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,z_{B})=\text{Tr}\left[q^{L_{0}-\frac{1}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{1}{24}}e^{2\pi iz_{B}\,j_{0}^{3}}e^{-2\pi i\bar{z}_{B}\,\bar{j}_{0}^{3}}\right]\ ,

(D.10)

see eq. (
B.2
), obeys the modular transformation (
D.2
) with

\hat{c}=\frac{\kappa-2}{2}\ .

(D.11)

According to the discussion of the previous section, we would then expect that
23
23

23

We denote by
(z_{B})_{1}
and
(z_{B})_{2}
respectively the real and imaginary part of
z_{B}
.

Z_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,z_{B})=e^{\frac{\pi(\kappa-2)}{\tau_{2}}(z_{B})_{1}^{2}}\,\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,z_{B})\ .

(D.12)

Unfortunately, we cannot verify eq. (
D.12
) directly, by computing left- and right-hand-side independently. In fact, we are not aware of a path integral derivation of the
SU(2)
partition function. However, a few consistency checks on eq. (
D.12
) can be made. For
\kappa=4
, the bosonic
SU(2)
WZW model is at level 2 and is equivalent to three free fermions transforming in the adjoint representation. In fact, making use of eq. (
3.34
) with
z=0
and
Y=0
, we reproduce eq. (
D.12
) with
\kappa=4
. Similarly, for
\kappa=3
the
SU(2)
WZW model is at level 1 and is equivalent to a compact boson. In fact, eq. (
D.11
) reduces to eq. (
D.6
).

From eq. (
2.56
) we find

Z_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,z_{B})=\int\mathcal{D}g_{su}\,e^{-(\kappa-2)S_{su}^{B}(g_{su},\,a[z_{B}],\,a[z_{B}]^{\dagger})}\\
=e^{\frac{\pi(\kappa-2)}{\tau_{2}}|z_{B}|^{2}}\int\mathcal{D}g_{su}\,{\rm exp}\left(-(\kappa-2)S^{\text{WZW}}(a[z_{B}]^{-\varepsilon}\,g_{su}\,a[z_{B}]^{\dagger\varepsilon})\right)\ ,

(D.13)

Equating eqs. (
D.12
) and (
D.13
) we obtain

\int\mathcal{D}g_{su}\,e^{-(\kappa-2)S^{\text{WZW}}(a[z_{B}]^{-\varepsilon}\,g_{su}\,a[z_{B}]^{\dagger\varepsilon})}=e^{-\frac{\pi(k-2)(z_{B})_{2}^{2}}{\tau_{2}}}\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,z_{B})\ ,

(D.14)

which will be used in the main text.

Asymmetric gauging

We are interested in generalizing (
D.14
) to the asymmetric embeddings

\varepsilon_{L}^{su}=-\ell\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}\ ,\qquad\varepsilon_{R}^{su}=-r\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}\ ,

(D.15)

with
\ell,r\in\mathds{R}
. This time, to relate the path integral and the trace partition function we cannot rely on modular invariance: this is supposed to hold only after assembling together all the factors involved in the gauging. However, we can proceed as follows. Let us assume that the correct generalization of (
D.12
) to asymmetric embeddings (
D.15
) reads

Z_{B,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,u)=e^{f(\kappa,\ell,r,\tau,u,\bar{u})}\,\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\ell\,u,r\,\bar{u})\ .

(D.16)

where
f(\kappa,\ell,r,\tau,u,\bar{u})
is some unknown function of
\ell,r,\tau,u,\bar{u}
and
\kappa
. In eq. (
D.16
), the left-hand-side is defined as

Z_{B,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,u)=\int\mathcal{D}g_{su}\,{\rm exp}\left(-(\kappa-2)S_{su}^{B}(g_{su},a[u],a[u]^{\dagger})\right)\ ,

(D.17)

with the embeddings (
D.15
), while by
\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\ell\,u,r\,\bar{u})
we denote the partition function (
B.2
) evaluated at
z_{B}=\ell\,u
and
\bar{z}_{B}=r\,\bar{u}
. Some useful information about the function
f(\kappa,\ell,r,u,\bar{u})
comes from requiring that at
\kappa=4
and
\kappa=3
eq. (
D.16
) respectively reproduces the free fermion and free boson asymmetric gauging results of Section
6
. For the free fermions, setting
Y=0
and
z=0
in eq. (
6.23
), we find

f(4,\ell,r,\tau,u,\bar{u})=\pi i(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}+\frac{\pi iu_{2}^{2}}{\tau_{2}^{2}}(\ell^{2}\tau-r^{2}\bar{\tau})+\frac{\pi(\ell^{2}+r^{2})|u|^{2}}{\tau_{2}}\ ,

(D.18)

Similarly, by setting
Y=0
in eq. (
6.28
) we obtain

f(3,\ell,r,\tau,u,\bar{u})=\frac{\pi i}{2}(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}+\frac{\pi iu_{2}^{2}}{2\tau_{2}^{2}}(\ell^{2}\tau-r^{2}\bar{\tau})+\frac{\pi(\ell^{2}+r^{2})|u|^{2}}{2\tau_{2}}=\frac{1}{2}f(4,\ell,r,\tau,u,\bar{u})\ .

(D.19)

In light of equations (
D.18
) and (
D.19
), we find it reasonable to assume that
f(\kappa,\ell,r,u,\bar{u})
is linear in
\kappa
and hence we deduce

Z_{B,\ell,r}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,u)=e^{\pi i\frac{\kappa-2}{2}(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}}\,e^{\frac{(\kappa-2)\pi\,i\,u_{2}^{2}}{2\tau_{2}^{2}}(\ell^{2}\tau-r^{2}\bar{\tau})}\,e^{\frac{\pi(\kappa-2)(\ell^{2}+r^{2})}{2\tau_{2}}|u|^{2}}\,\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\ell\,u,r\,\bar{u})\ .

(D.20)

Using eq. (
2.56
) with

(\kappa-2)S^{X,Y}_{su}=-\frac{\pi(\kappa-2)}{2\tau_{2}}(\ell^{2}+r^{2})|u|^{2}\ ,

(D.21)

we finally obtain

\int\mathcal{D}g_{su}\,e^{-S^{\text{WZW}}(a[u]^{-\varepsilon_{L}^{su}}\,g_{su}\,a[u]^{\dagger\varepsilon_{R}^{su}})}=e^{\pi i\frac{\kappa-2}{2}(\ell^{2}-r^{2})s_{1}s_{2}}\,e^{\frac{(\kappa-2)\pi\,i\,u_{2}^{2}}{2\tau_{2}^{2}}(\ell^{2}\tau-r^{2}\bar{\tau})}\,\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\ell\,u,r\,\bar{u})\ .

(D.22)

Appendix E
GWZW in superspace

Following
Figueroa-OFarrill:1996xek
, in this appendix we expand in components the
\mathcal{N}=1
GWZW action and extend their analysis to asymmetric and null gauging.

E.1
Symmetric gauging

The
\mathcal{N}=1
vector GWZW action for a simple group
G
is defined by promoting the fields in (
2.8
) to superfields
Abdalla:1984ef
;
DiVecchia:1984nyg
;
Figueroa-OFarrill:1996xek
,

I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=I[\mathbb{G}]+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\text{d}\theta\text{d}\bar{\theta}\int\text{d}^{2}v\,\text{Tr}\left(-\mathbb{J}\bar{\mathbb{A}}-\mathbb{A}\bar{\mathbb{J}}-\mathbb{A}\bar{\mathbb{A}}+\mathbb{A}\mathbb{G}^{-1}\bar{\mathbb{A}}\mathbb{G}\right)\ ,

(E.1)

where
I[\mathbb{G}]
is the
\mathcal{N}=1
WZW action, also defined by promoting the fields in (
2.1
) to superfields. Once decomposed into components it reads
Figueroa-OFarrill:1996xek

I[\mathbb{G}]=S^{\text{WZW}}(g)+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\psi\bar{\nabla}\psi+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\bar{\psi}\nabla\bar{\psi}\ ,

(E.2)

where covariant derivatives are defined by

\nabla=\partial_{v}-[\partial_{v}gg^{-1},\cdot\ ]\ ,\qquad\bar{\nabla}=\partial_{\bar{v}}+[g^{-1}\partial_{\bar{v}}g,\cdot\ ]\ ,

(E.3)

and here and in the following
[\ \,,\ \,]
denotes the generalised commutator,
i.e.

[\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2}]=\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}-\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{1}
when
\mathcal{O}_{1}
or
\mathcal{O}_{2}
are Grassmann even and
[\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2}]=\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}+\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{1}
when both
\mathcal{O}_{1}
and
\mathcal{O}_{2}
are Grassmann odd.
We parametrize the superfield
\mathbb{G}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}

\displaystyle=g+\theta g\psi+\bar{\theta}\bar{\psi}g+\theta\bar{\theta}(ga-g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g)\ ,

(E.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{-1}

\displaystyle=g^{-1}-\theta\psi g^{-1}-\bar{\theta}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}-\theta\bar{\theta}(ag^{-1}-g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g\psi g^{-1})\ .

(E.5)

The supercurrents read

\displaystyle\mathbb{J}

\displaystyle=-D\mathbb{G}\mathbb{G}^{-1}=-g\psi g^{-1}-\theta(\partial_{v}gg^{-1}+g\psi^{2}g^{-1})-\bar{\theta}gag^{-1}-\theta\bar{\theta}(\nabla\bar{\psi}+g[a,\psi]g^{-1})\ ,

(E.6)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{J}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}^{-1}\bar{D}\mathbb{G}=g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g-\theta a+\bar{\theta}(g^{-1}\partial_{\bar{v}}g-g^{-1}\bar{\psi}^{2}g)-\theta\bar{\theta}(\bar{\nabla}\psi+[a,g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g])\ ,

(E.7)

where we introduced the usual superderivatives

D=\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}+\theta\partial_{v}\ ,\qquad\bar{D}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\theta}}+\bar{\theta}\partial_{\bar{v}}\ .

(E.8)

Let us parametrize the super gauge field as

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}

\displaystyle=\rho+\theta A+\bar{\theta}\bar{B}+\theta\bar{\theta}\lambda\ ,

(E.9a)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\rho}+\theta B+\bar{\theta}\bar{A}+\theta\bar{\theta}\bar{\lambda}\ .

(E.9b)

Expanding in components and integrating out auxiliary fields, the action (
E.1
) can be rewritten as
Figueroa-OFarrill:1996xek

I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=S^{V}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\left(\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\,\partial_{\bar{v}}\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}-\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\,[\mathcal{\bar{A}},\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}]+\bar{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\,\partial_{v}\bar{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}-\bar{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\,[\mathcal{A},\bar{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}]\right)\ ,

(E.10)

where we defined

\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}=g(\psi+\rho)g^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\ ,\qquad\bar{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}=g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho})g|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\ ,

(E.11)

and

\mathcal{A}=A-\rho^{2}\ ,\qquad\bar{\mathcal{A}}=\bar{A}-\bar{\rho}^{2}\ ,

(E.12)

while
I_{B}[g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}}]
denotes the bosonic vector gauged WZW action

S^{V}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})=S^{\text{WZW}}(g)+\frac{1}{\pi}\int\text{d}^{2}v\,\text{Tr}(\partial_{v}gg^{-1}\bar{\mathcal{A}}-\mathcal{A}g^{-1}\partial_{\bar{v}}g-\mathcal{A}\bar{\mathcal{A}}+\mathcal{A}g^{-1}\bar{\mathcal{A}}g)\ .

(E.13)

In eqs. (
E.10
) and (
E.11
),
\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}
denotes the orthogonal complement of
\mathfrak{h}
,

\mathfrak{h}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}\ ,

(E.14)

and
e.g.
the notation
\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}
denotes the projection of
\Psi
onto
\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}
.

E.2
Asymmetric gauging

Let us now repeat the analysis of the previous section for asymmetric gauging. The asymmetric
\mathcal{N}=1
GWZW action reads

I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=I[\mathbb{G}]-\int\text{d}\theta\text{d}\bar{\theta}\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(\mathbb{J}\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{L}+\mathbb{A}_{R}\bar{\mathbb{J}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\mathbb{A}_{L}\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{L}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\mathbb{A}_{R}\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{R}-\mathbb{A}_{R}\mathbb{G}^{-1}\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{L}\mathbb{G}\Bigr)\ ,

(E.15)

where in order to lighten the notation we omitted the sum over components entering
e.g.
eq. (
2.19
).
The short-hand notation
\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{L,R}
and
\mathbb{A}_{L,R}
stands for

\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{L,R}\equiv\epsilon_{L,R}(\bar{\mathbb{A}})\ ,\qquad\mathbb{A}_{L,R}\equiv\epsilon_{L,R}(\mathbb{A})\ ,

(E.16)

and we are still parametrizing the super gauge field as in eq. (
E.9
). Similarly, we write

A_{L,R}\equiv\epsilon_{L,R}(A)\ ,\qquad B_{L,R}\equiv\epsilon_{L,R}(B)\ ,\qquad\rho_{L,R}\equiv\epsilon_{L,R}(\rho)\ ,\qquad\bar{\rho}_{L,R}\equiv\epsilon_{L,R}(\bar{\rho})\ ,

(E.17)

and so on. Expanding in components the various terms entering the action (
E.15
) we find

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},

\displaystyle\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=I[\mathbb{G}]+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(\partial_{v}gg^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}-A_{R}g^{-1}\partial_{\bar{v}}g-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}A_{L}\bar{A}_{L}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}A_{R}\bar{A}_{R}+A_{R}g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}g

\displaystyle+A_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}^{2}g-A_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}\bar{\psi}g-A_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}g+g\psi^{2}g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}g\psi+\rho_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}g

\displaystyle-\lambda_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\lambda_{L}\bar{\rho}_{L}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\lambda_{R}\bar{\rho}_{R}+\lambda_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\rho_{L}\bar{\lambda}_{L}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\rho_{R}\bar{\lambda}_{R}+g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\lambda}_{L}+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\lambda}_{L}g

\displaystyle+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\bar{B}_{L}B_{L}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\bar{B}_{R}B_{R}+\rho_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}\bar{\psi}g-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}B_{L}g+\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi-\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}g+\bar{B}_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g

\displaystyle+\rho_{R}\bar{\nabla}\psi+\nabla\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g-\rho_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}\bar{\psi}g+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g

\displaystyle-\bar{B}_{R}a+\rho_{R}[a,g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g]-gag^{-1}B_{L}+g[a,\psi]g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}ga-\rho_{R}ag^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\Bigr)\ .

(E.18)

The bosonic asymmetrically gauged WZW action reads, see eq. (
2.19
),

S^{B}(g,A,\bar{A})=S^{\text{WZW}}(g)+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{\pi}\,\text{Tr}(\partial_{v}gg^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}-A_{R}g^{-1}\partial_{\bar{v}}g-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}A_{L}\bar{A}_{L}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}A_{R}\bar{A}_{R}+A_{R}g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}g)\ ,

(E.19)

so that the terms in the first line of (
E.18
) can be rewritten as

S^{B}(g,A,\bar{A})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\psi\bar{\nabla}\psi+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\bar{\psi}\nabla\bar{\psi}\ .

(E.20)

The terms in the second line of (
E.18
) can be reorganized as

-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}\,[gA_{R}g^{-1},\bar{\psi}]-[gA_{R}g^{-1},\bar{\psi}]\bar{\rho}_{L}-\frac{1}{2}\psi\,[g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}g,\psi]-\rho_{R}[g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}g,\psi]\ .

(E.21)

Integrating out the auxiliary field
a
we find the constraint

\bar{B}_{R}+g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g\rho_{R}+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g+g^{-1}B_{L}g-\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g-g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g-g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\rho_{R}=0\ .

(E.22)

With all these simplifications eq. (
E.18
) becomes

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=

\displaystyle\,S^{B}(g,A,\bar{A})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(\,\tfrac{1}{2}\psi\bar{\nabla}\psi-\tfrac{1}{2}\psi\,[g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}g,\psi]+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}\nabla\bar{\psi}-\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}\,[gA_{R}g^{-1},\bar{\psi}]

\displaystyle+\rho_{R}\bar{\nabla}\psi-\rho_{R}[g^{-1}\bar{A}_{L}g,\psi]+\nabla\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}-[gA_{R}g^{-1},\bar{\psi}]\bar{\rho}_{L}

\displaystyle-\lambda_{R}\bigl(\bar{\rho}_{R}+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\bigr)-\bigl(\rho_{L}-g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bigr)\bar{\lambda}_{L}

\displaystyle+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\bar{B}_{L}B_{L}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\bar{B}_{R}B_{R}+\rho_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}\bar{\psi}g-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}B_{L}g

\displaystyle+\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi-\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}g+\bar{B}_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g

\displaystyle-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g-\rho_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}\bar{\psi}g+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\Bigr)\ ,

(E.23)

where we used that, by the anomaly cancellation condition (
2.21
),

\text{Tr}\left(\lambda_{L}\bar{\rho}_{L}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(\lambda_{R}\bar{\rho}_{R}\right)\ ,\qquad\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{L}\bar{\lambda}_{L}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{R}\bar{\lambda}_{R}\right)\ .

(E.24)

Adding to the action (
E.23
) the following terms

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\psi[g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g,\psi]+\psi^{2}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g\ ,

(E.25a)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}[g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1},\bar{\psi}]+\bar{\psi}^{2}g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\ ,

(E.25b)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\rho_{R}[g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g,\psi]-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g\psi+\rho_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g\ ,

(E.25c)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=[g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1},\bar{\psi}]\bar{\rho}_{L}-g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}+\bar{\psi}g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}\ ,

(E.25d)

introducing the covariant derivatives

\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}=\nabla-[g\mathcal{A}_{R}g^{-1},\ ]\ ,\qquad\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}=\bar{\nabla}-[g^{-1}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}g,\ ]\ ,

(E.26)

and the gauge fields

\mathcal{A}_{L,R}=A_{L,R}-\rho^{2}_{L,R}\ ,\qquad\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L,R}=\bar{A}_{L,R}-\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L,R}\ ,

(E.27)

we can rewrite the action (
E.23
) as

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]

\displaystyle=S^{B}(g,A,\bar{A})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(\,\tfrac{1}{2}\psi\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\psi+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\psi}+\rho_{R}\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\psi+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}

\displaystyle-\lambda_{R}\bigl(\bar{\rho}_{R}+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\bigr)-\bigl(\rho_{L}-g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bigr)\bar{\lambda}_{L}

\displaystyle-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g-\rho_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}\bar{\psi}g+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g

\displaystyle+\psi^{2}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g+\bar{\psi}^{2}g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g\psi+\rho_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g-g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}+\bar{\psi}g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{B}_{L}B_{L}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{B}_{R}B_{R}+\rho_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}\bar{\psi}g-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}B_{L}g

\displaystyle+\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi-\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}g+\bar{B}_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\Bigr)\ .

(E.28)

Using eq. (
E.22
) we find

\displaystyle\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}g

\displaystyle=-\bar{\psi}g\rho_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}-\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}B_{L}g-B^{2}_{L}+\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}B_{L}g+\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi g^{-1}B_{L}

\displaystyle\quad+\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}B_{L}g+\bar{\rho}_{L}g\rho_{R}g^{-1}B_{L}

(E.29)

\displaystyle=-\bar{B}^{2}_{R}-\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g\rho_{R}-\bar{B}_{R}\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g+\bar{B}_{R}\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g+\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi

\displaystyle\quad+\bar{B}_{R}\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g+\bar{B}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\rho_{R}\ .

(E.30)

We can then rewrite the last two lines of (
E.28
) as

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}B^{2}_{L}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{B}^{2}_{R}+\tfrac{1}{2}B_{L}\bar{B}_{L}+\tfrac{1}{2}B_{R}\bar{B}_{R}+\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g,\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1},\bar{\rho}_{L}]

\displaystyle+\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi^{2}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}-g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g\rho_{R}-\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}+\bar{\rho}_{L}g\psi\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}

\displaystyle-\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}\bar{\psi}g\rho_{R}+\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g\rho_{R}-\psi g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}g\rho_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}g

\displaystyle+\bar{\psi}g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}-\bar{\psi}g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}-\bar{\rho}_{L}g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\psi}+\bar{\rho}_{L}g\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}_{L}\ .

(E.31)

Many terms cancel against opposite terms in the third and fourth line of (
E.28
) and the supersymmetric action reduces to

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]

\displaystyle=\,S^{B}(g,A,\bar{A})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(\,\tfrac{1}{2}\psi\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\psi+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\psi}+\rho_{R}\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\psi+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\psi}\bar{\rho}_{L}-\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g

\displaystyle\qquad-(\rho_{L}-g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1})\bar{\lambda}_{L}-\lambda_{R}(\bar{\rho}_{R}+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}B_{L}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{B}_{R}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}B_{L}\bar{B}_{L}+\tfrac{1}{2}B_{R}\bar{B}_{R}+\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g,\rho_{R}]-B_{L}[g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\Bigr)\ .

(E.32)

The terms in the second line of (
E.32
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle S^{B}(g,A,\bar{A})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{R}\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\rho_{R}-\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\rho}_{L}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\rho}_{L}-\rho^{2}_{R}g^{-1}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}g

\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt+\tfrac{1}{2}(\psi+\rho_{R})\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}(\psi+\rho_{R})+\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})\Bigr)

(E.33a)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(-\rho_{L}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\rho_{L}-\bar{\rho}_{R}\partial_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{L}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}-\rho_{R}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{R}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.0pt+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\left(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\right)+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\left(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\right)\Bigr)\ ,

(E.33b)

where we expressed
S^{B}(g,A,\bar{A})
in terms of
S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})
, see eq. (
E.27
), and
\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}
,
\nabla_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}
in terms of
\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}
,
\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}
,

\partial_{\mathcal{A}}=\partial_{v}-[\mathcal{A},\cdot\ ]\ ,\qquad\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}=\partial_{\bar{v}}-[\bar{\mathcal{A}},\cdot\ ]\ .

(E.34)

We also used that by eq. (
2.21
)

\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{R}\partial_{\bar{v}}\rho_{R}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{L}\partial_{\bar{v}}\rho_{L}\right)\ ,

(E.35)

and

\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{L}^{2}\bar{A}_{L}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(\rho^{2}_{R}\bar{A}_{R}\right)\ ,\qquad\text{Tr}\left(A_{R}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{R}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(A_{L}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}\right)\ ,

(E.36)

In fact, notice that
\epsilon_{L}
,
\epsilon_{R}
are Lie algebra homomorphisms,
i.e.
linear maps preserving the Lie braket and hence

\epsilon_{L}(\rho^{2})=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{L}([\rho,\rho])=\frac{1}{2}[\epsilon_{L}(\rho),\epsilon_{L}(\rho)]=\epsilon_{L}(\rho)^{2}=\rho_{L}^{2}\ .

(E.37)

The action (
E.32
) thus becomes

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=\,

\displaystyle S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\Bigl(-\rho_{L}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\rho_{L}-\bar{\rho}_{R}\partial_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{L}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}-\rho_{R}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{R}

\displaystyle+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\left(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\right)+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\left(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\right)

\displaystyle-2(\rho_{L}-g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1})\bar{\lambda}_{L}-2\lambda_{R}(\bar{\rho}_{R}+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g)

\displaystyle+B_{L}^{2}+\bar{B}_{R}^{2}+B_{L}\bar{B}_{L}+B_{R}\bar{B}_{R}

\displaystyle+2\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g,\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\Bigr)\ .

(E.38)

We now decompose
\mathfrak{g}
as

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}_{L}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}=\mathfrak{h}_{R}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}\ .

(E.39)

Notice that this decomposition is not always possible. In fact, when the gauging is null, we have
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}\supset\mathfrak{h}_{L}
and eq. (
E.39
) cannot possibly hold. A sufficient condition for eq. (
E.39
) to hold is requiring that
\mathfrak{h}_{L}
is
anisotropic

(
nacinovich
, Theorem 4.1)
. A vector subspace
\mathfrak{h}_{L}
of a vector space
\mathfrak{g}
is said to be anisotropic with respect to a symmetric bilinear form
\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle
if for each non-zero vector
v\in\mathfrak{h}_{L}
there exist a vector
w\in\mathfrak{h}_{L}
such that
\langle v,w\rangle\neq 0
. In the rest of this subsection, we will assume
\mathfrak{h}_{L}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{R}
to be anisotropic, so that eq. (
E.39
) holds. In the next subsection we will then separately consider the case of null gauging.

Integrating out
\lambda
and
\bar{\lambda}
, we obtain the equations of motion

\rho_{L}=g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}}\ ,\qquad\bar{\rho}_{R}=-g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}}\ .

(E.40)

Making use of eq. (
E.40
), the terms involving
B
and
\bar{B}
in the last two lines of (
E.38
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle 2\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}}+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}},\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}}+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}},\bar{\rho}_{L}]

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 2\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}},\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}},\bar{\rho}_{L}]

(E.41)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle-2\bar{B}_{R}[\bar{\rho}_{R},\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[\rho_{L},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\ .

(E.42)

In the second equality we used that

\text{Tr}\left(\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}+\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}},\rho_{R}]\right)=0

(E.43)

and a similar condition for the term involving
B_{L}
. Let us explain how this comes about. Using the properties of the trace,

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\left(\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}},\rho_{R}]\right)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}\left(-\rho_{R}\,\bar{B}_{R}\,g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}}+\bar{B}_{R}\,\rho_{R}\,g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}}\right)

(E.44)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}\left([\bar{B}_{R},\rho_{R}]g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}}\right)=0\ .

(E.45)

In fact,
[\bar{B}_{R},\rho_{R}]\in\mathfrak{h}_{R}
since
\epsilon_{R}
is a Lie algebra homomorphism. The action (
E.38
) thus becomes

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=

\displaystyle\,S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(-\rho_{L}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\rho_{L}-\bar{\rho}_{R}\partial_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{L}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}-\rho_{R}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{R}

\displaystyle+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\left(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\right)+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\left(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\right)

\displaystyle+B_{L}^{2}+\bar{B}_{R}^{2}+B_{L}\bar{B}_{L}+B_{R}\bar{B}_{R}-2\bar{B}_{R}[\bar{\rho}_{R},\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[\rho_{L},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\Bigr)\ .

(E.46)

Finally, using again eq. (
2.21
) and invariance of the Lie braket under
\epsilon_{L,R}
we can rewrite the last line in (
E.46
) as

(B_{L}+\bar{B}_{L})(B_{L}+\bar{B}_{L})-2(B_{L}+\bar{B}_{L})[\rho_{L},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\ .

(E.47)

Integrating out
B_{L}+\bar{B}_{L}
we find

B_{L}+\bar{B}_{L}=[\rho_{L},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\ ,

(E.48)

and replacing it back into the action, we have

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=

\displaystyle\,S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(-\rho_{L}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\rho_{L}-\bar{\rho}_{R}\partial_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\rho}_{R}

\displaystyle+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\left(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\right)+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\left(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\right)\Bigr)\ ,

(E.49)

where we used that

\text{Tr}\left(\rho^{2}_{L}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(\rho^{2}_{R}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{R}\right)\ ,

(E.50)

again following from (
2.21
) and invariance of the Lie bracket.

We now wish to show that the last two terms on the first line of (
E.49
) cancel the part of the terms in the last line that are in
\mathfrak{h}
.
Decomposing
g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}
and
g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g
as

\displaystyle g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}

\displaystyle=\rho_{L}+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}}\ ,

(E.51)

\displaystyle g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g

\displaystyle=-\rho_{R}+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}}\ ,

(E.52)

the action takes the final form

I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Biggl(\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}}+\bar{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}}\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\bar{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}}\Biggr)\ ,

(E.53)

where we introduced

\Psi=g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\ ,\qquad\bar{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}=g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\ ,

(E.54)

and used that
e.g.

\text{Tr}\left(\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}}[\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L},\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}}]\right)=\text{Tr}\left([\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}},{\mathcal{A}}_{L}]\,\Psi|_{\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}}\right)=0\ .

(E.55)

E.3
Null gauging

We already noticed that when the gauging is null, it is not possible to decompose
\mathfrak{g}
as in (
E.39
). We should then restart from eq. (
E.38
) and use that by eq. (
2.31
) we have

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{L}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\rho_{L}\right)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{L}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{L}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{L}\bar{\lambda}_{L}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(B_{L}^{2}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(B_{L}\bar{B}_{L}\right)=0\ ,

(E.56)

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\left(\bar{\rho}_{R}\partial_{\mathcal{A}_{R}}\bar{\rho}_{R}\right)

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}\left(\rho_{R}^{2}\bar{\rho}^{2}_{R}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(\lambda_{R}\bar{\rho}_{R}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(\bar{B}_{R}^{2}\right)=\text{Tr}\left(B_{R}\bar{B}_{R}\right)=0\ .

Eq. (
E.38
) reduces to

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=

\displaystyle\,S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(2g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bar{\lambda}_{L}-2\lambda_{R}\,g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g

\displaystyle+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\left(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\right)+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\left(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\right)

\displaystyle+2\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g,\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\Bigr)\ .

(E.57)

Let us now decompose
\mathfrak{g}
as

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\notin}=\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\notin}\ ,

(E.58)

where
e.g.

\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}
is defined as usual,

\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}=\{v\in\mathfrak{g}\,|\,\langle v,w\rangle=0\ \forall\,w\in\mathfrak{h}_{L}\}\ ,

(E.59)

while
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\notin}
is constructed by completing a basis of
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}
to a basis of
\mathfrak{g}
.
Similarly for
\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\notin}
. Notice that, by construction, this decomposition of
\mathfrak{g}
is always possible. When the gauging is not null, we have
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\notin}=\mathfrak{h}_{L}
, while for null gauging we have
\mathfrak{h}_{L}\subseteq\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}
.

Let us decompose the fermions as

\displaystyle g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}

\displaystyle=g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\perp}}+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\notin}}\ ,

(E.60)

\displaystyle g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g

\displaystyle=g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\perp}}+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\notin}}\ ,

(E.61)

By definition of
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}
,

\text{Tr}\left(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\perp}}\,\bar{\lambda}_{L}\right)=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad\text{Tr}\left(\lambda_{R}\,g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\perp}}\right)=0\ ,

(E.62)

so that the null gauged action (
E.57
) becomes

\displaystyle I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=

\displaystyle\,S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(2g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\notin}}\,\bar{\lambda}_{L}-2\lambda_{R}\,g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\notin}}

\displaystyle+g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\left(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}\right)+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\,\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\left(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g\right)

\displaystyle+2\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g,\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\Bigr)\ .

(E.63)

Integrating out
\lambda
and
\bar{\lambda}
we find

g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\notin}}=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\notin}}=0\ .

(E.64)

The second line of eq. (
E.63
) then simplifies to

g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\perp}}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\left(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\perp}}\right)+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\perp}}\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\left(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\perp}}\right)\ ,

(E.65)

while the last line of eq. (
E.63
) vanishes,

\text{Tr}\Bigl(2\bar{B}_{R}[g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\perp}},\rho_{R}]-2B_{L}[g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\perp}},\bar{\rho}_{L}]\Bigr)\\
=\text{Tr}\Bigl(2\underbrace{[\bar{B}_{R},\rho_{R}]}_{~~~\in\,\mathfrak{h}_{R}}\,g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\perp}}-2\underbrace{[B_{L},\bar{\rho}_{L}]}_{~~~\in\,\mathfrak{h}_{L}}\,g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\perp}}\Bigr)=0\ .

(E.66)

The action (
E.63
) thus collapses to

I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\perp}}\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\bigl(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{h_{L}^{\perp}}\bigr)\\
+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\perp}}\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\bigl(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{h_{R}^{\perp}}\bigr)\Bigr)\ .

(E.67)

This is exactly the same result we obtained in the previous Section,
c.f.
eq. (
E.53
). The difference is that when the gauging is null

\mathfrak{h}_{L}\subseteq\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}\ ,\qquad\mathfrak{h}_{R}\subseteq\mathfrak{h}_{R}^{\perp}\ ,

(E.68)

and hence the worldsheet fermions taking values in
\mathfrak{h}_{L}
also decouple from the action (as they must – they are shifted linearly by a fermionic gauge transformation; thus they can be set to zero as a gauge choice).

The Lie algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
is a direct sum of
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}
and its complement
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\notin}
, see eq. (
E.58
);
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}
further decomposes into
\mathfrak{h}_{L}
and its complement in
\mathfrak{h}_{L}^{\perp}
which we denote
{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}^{{\sf t}}
and refer to as the “transverse space”. We thus have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathfrak{g}}&={\mathfrak{h}}_{L}^{\perp}\oplus{\mathfrak{h}}_{L}^{\notin}={\mathfrak{h}}_{L}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}^{\sf t}\oplus{\mathfrak{h}}_{L}^{\notin}\ ,\\
{\mathfrak{g}}&={\mathfrak{h}}_{R}^{\perp}\oplus{\mathfrak{h}}_{R}^{\notin}={\mathfrak{h}}_{R}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{R}^{\sf t}\oplus{\mathfrak{h}}_{R}^{\notin}\ .\end{split}

(E.69)

The contributions of
{\mathfrak{h}}_{L}
(respectively
{\mathfrak{h}}_{R}
) to the kinetic terms vanish due to the null condition (
2.31
) and orthogonality to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}^{\sf t}
(respectively
{\mathfrak{g}}_{R}^{\sf t}
); thus we have

I[\mathbb{G},\mathbb{A},\bar{\mathbb{A}}]=S^{B}(g,\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathcal{A}})+\int\frac{\text{d}^{2}v}{2\pi}\,\text{Tr}\Bigl(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}^{\sf t}}\,\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{L}}\bigl(g(\psi+\rho_{R})g^{-1}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}^{\sf t}}\,\bigr)\\
+g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{R}^{\sf t}}\,\partial_{{\mathcal{A}}_{R}}\bigl(g^{-1}(\bar{\psi}-\bar{\rho}_{L})g|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{R}^{\sf t}}\,\bigr)\Bigr)\ .

(E.70)

Hence the fermion kinetic term reduces to the “transverse space” under null gauging. The last step consists of decoupling the free fermions from the gauge group
G
. This has the effect of shifting the level in front of the bosonic action by the dual Coxeter number
DiVecchia:1984nyg
;
Goddard:1986bp
;
Giveon:1998ns
;
Ferreira:2017pgt
.

The above treatment is suitable for simple cosets such as those discussed in Sections
3
and
4
, where the transverse space
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\sf t}
sits simply in
{\mathfrak{g}}
and one retains the fermions
\psi^{\pm}
while removing the rest from the path integral. However, the situation is a good deal more complicated when one comes to examples such as the supertube coset (
6.1
); now the removed fermions are some particular linear combinations of
\psi^{3}_{sl},\psi^{3}_{su},\psi_{t},\psi_{y}
, and the retained ones some other linear combinations. Furthermore, this procedure suffers from a lack of manifest supersymmetry in intermediate steps of the calculation.

Writing down the partition function for these fermions is a trivial matter, since they don’t couple to the gauge group and yield simply a free fermion partition function (
6.24
). However, for any computation of operator correlators it is simpler to retain
all
of the fermions and impose a BRST constraint to eliminate redundant degrees of freedom.

One proceeds as follows. Instead of integrating over the fermionic Lagrange multipliers
\lambda,\bar{\lambda}
in the gauge multiplet to obtain the constraints (
E.40
), one maintains manifest supersymmetry by writing them as

\lambda=-\partial\chi\ ,\qquad\bar{\lambda}=-\bar{\partial}\chi\ ,

(E.71)

analogous to the Hodge decomposition of the gauge fields (
2.40
). The Jacobian for this transformation in the functional measure then cancels against the functional determinant of the extra pair of fermions one gets because the constraint (
E.64
) is not imposed as a delta function in the path integral. In the end, one arrives at the same result obtained from the above procedure of eliminating one fermion via the fermionic gauge constraint and another via gauge fixing.

The Jacobian can be represented via a path integral over spinor ghosts
\beta,\gamma
which are superpartners of the gauge field ghosts
b,c
, and there is an additional term
\gamma\zeta
in the BRST current, where
\zeta
is the superpartner of the gauge current
\mathcal{J}
(and similarly for the right-movers). The role of this term is to ensure that physical operators only contain fermions living in the transverse space
{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\sf t}
(with corresponding constraints on spin fields, as discussed in
Bufalini:2021ndn
;
Martinec:2022okx
).

Appendix F
Periodicity properties of the supertube partition function

In this appendix we derive some periodicity properties of the supertube partition function that are used in the main text. In particular, we are going to show that the integrand
\mathcal{I}(\tau,u,z)
introduced in eq. (
6.2.3
) is periodic in the integration variable
u
with periods
\tau
and
1
,
i.e.

\mathcal{I}(u+m_{1}\tau+m_{2},\tau,z)=\mathcal{I}(u,\tau,z)\ ,\qquad\forall\,m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathds{Z}\ .

(F.1)

Equivalently, in terms of the holonomies
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
introduced in eq. (
2.42
),

\mathcal{I}(s_{1}+m_{1},s_{2}+m_{2},\tau,z)=\mathcal{I}(s_{1},s_{2},\tau,z)\ ,\qquad\forall\,m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathds{Z}\ .

(F.2)

Let us analyse the behavior of the various factors entering (
6.2.3
). It is easy to check that

e^{-\pi n_{5}\tau_{2}(s_{1}+m_{1})^{2}}e^{-4\pi z_{2}(s_{1}+m_{1})}=e^{-\pi n_{5}\tau_{2}m_{1}^{2}}\,e^{-2\pi n_{5}u_{2}m_{1}}\,e^{-4\pi z_{2}m_{1}}\,e^{-\pi n_{5}\tau_{2}s_{1}^{2}}e^{-4\pi z_{2}s_{1}}\ ,

(F.3)

and that

\left|\frac{\theta_{1}(\tau,u+\frac{n_{5}+2}{n_{5}}z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})}{\theta_{1}(\tau,u+\frac{2}{n_{5}}z+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})}\right|^{2}=e^{4\pi m_{1}z_{2}}\left|\frac{\theta_{1}(\tau,u+\frac{n_{5}+2}{n_{5}}z)}{\theta_{1}(\tau,u+\frac{2}{n_{5}}z)}\right|^{2}\ ,

(F.4)

where we used the periodicity properties of
\theta_{1}
, see eq. (
A.8
). Since
\ell_{2},r_{2}\in\mathds{Z}
, see Section
6.1
, eq. (
A.8
) also implies

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(\tau,\ell_{2}u+\tfrac{n_{5}-2}{n_{5}}z+\ell_{2}(m_{1}\tau+m_{2}))

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt=(-1)^{\ell_{2}(m_{1}+m_{2})}\,q^{-\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}m_{1}^{2}}{2}}\,e^{-2\pi i\ell_{2}^{2}m_{1}u}\,e^{-2\pi i\ell_{2}m_{1}\frac{n_{5}-2}{n_{5}}z}\,\theta_{1}(\tau,\ell_{2}u+\tfrac{n_{5}-2}{n_{5}}z)\ ,

(F.5)

\displaystyle\overline{\theta_{1}(\tau,r_{2}u+\tfrac{n_{5}-2}{n_{5}}z+r_{2}(m_{1}\tau+m_{2}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt=(-1)^{r_{2}(m_{1}+m_{2})}\,\bar{q}^{-\frac{r_{2}^{2}m_{1}^{2}}{2}}\,e^{2\pi ir_{2}^{2}m_{1}\bar{u}}\,e^{2\pi ir_{2}m_{1}\frac{n_{5}-2}{n_{5}}\bar{z}}\,\overline{\theta_{1}(\tau,r_{2}u+\tfrac{n_{5}-2}{n_{5}}z)}\ .

(F.6)

The
\mathds{R}_{t}
contribution transforms as

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}E\,q^{-\frac{E^{2}}{4}}\,\bar{q}^{-\frac{E^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-2\pi\ell_{3}E(u_{2}+m_{1}\tau_{2})}=q^{\frac{m_{1}^{2}\ell_{3}^{2}}{4}}\,\bar{q}^{\frac{m_{1}^{2}\ell_{3}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-2\pi\ell_{3}^{2}m_{1}u_{2}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\text{d}E\,q^{-\frac{E^{2}}{4}}\,\bar{q}^{-\frac{E^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-2\pi\ell_{3}Eu_{2}}\ .

(F.7)

Using the quantization conditions (
6.6
), one sees that the
U(1)_{y}
partition function obeys

\displaystyle\sum_{w,n\in\mathds{Z}}e^{\frac{\pi i}{2}\tau(\frac{n}{R}+wR)^{2}}\,e^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\bar{\tau}(\frac{n}{R}-wR)^{2}}\,e^{\pi i\ell_{4}(\frac{n}{R}+wR)(u+m_{1}\tau+m_{2})}\,e^{-\pi ir_{4}(\frac{n}{R}-wR)(\bar{u}+m_{1}\bar{\tau}+m_{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt=q^{-\frac{\ell_{4}^{2}\,m_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,\bar{q}^{-\frac{r_{4}^{2}\,m_{1}^{2}}{4}}\,e^{-\pi i\ell_{4}^{2}m_{1}u}\,e^{\pi ir_{4}^{2}m_{1}u}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\times\,\sum_{w,n\in\mathds{Z}}e^{\frac{\pi i}{2}\tau(\frac{n}{R}+wR)^{2}}\,e^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\bar{\tau}(\frac{n}{R}-wR)^{2}}\,e^{\pi i\ell_{4}(\frac{n}{R}+wR)u}\,e^{-\pi ir_{4}(\frac{n}{R}-wR)\bar{u}}\ .

(F.8)

Finally, making use of the fact that
l_{2}-r_{2}=2{\mathsf{n}}\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}
together with (
B.6
) and (
B.9
), we obtain

\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}\left(\tau,\bar{\tau},\ell_{2}u-\tfrac{2}{n_{5}}z+\ell_{2}(m_{1}\tau+m_{2}),r_{2}\bar{u}-\tfrac{2}{n_{5}}\bar{z}+r_{2}(m_{1}\tau+m_{2})\right)\\
=q^{-\frac{(n_{5}-2)}{4}\ell_{2}^{2}m_{1}^{2}}\,\bar{q}^{-\frac{(n_{5}-2)}{4}r_{2}^{2}m_{1}^{2}}\,e^{-\pi i(n_{5}-2)\ell_{2}^{2}m_{1}u}\,e^{\pi i(n_{5}-2)r_{2}^{2}m_{1}\bar{u}}\,e^{2\pi i\frac{n_{5}-2}{n_{5}}\ell_{2}m_{1}z}\,e^{-2\pi i\frac{n_{5}-2}{n_{5}}r_{2}m_{1}\bar{z}}\\
\times\,\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}\left(\tau,\bar{\tau},\ell_{2}u-\tfrac{2}{n_{5}}z,r_{2}\bar{u}-\tfrac{2}{n_{5}}\bar{z}\right)\ .

(F.9)

Using the null constraints (
6.3
) it is now easy to derive eq. (
F.1
).

The periodicity property (
F.1
) of the integrand (
6.2.3
) implies the following identity for the integral,

\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{2}\,\mathcal{I}(\tau,u,z)=\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{2}\,\mathcal{I}(\tau,u-a\tau-b,z)\ ,\qquad\forall\,a,b\in\mathds{R}\ .

(F.10)

In the special case of
a,b\in\mathds{Z}
, eq. (
F.10
) directly follows from (
F.1
). Let us derive (
F.10
) for
a
,
b
arbitrary real numbers. We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle\text{d}s_{1}\,\mathcal{I}(u)=\int_{a}^{1+a}\text{d}s_{1}\,\mathcal{I}((s_{1}-a)\tau+s_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{1}\,\mathcal{I}((s_{1}-a)\tau+s_{2})-\int_{0}^{a}\text{d}s_{1}\,\mathcal{I}((s_{1}-a)\tau+s_{2})+\int_{0}^{a}\text{d}s_{1}\,\mathcal{I}((s_{1}-a+1)\tau+s_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{1}\,\mathcal{I}((s_{1}-a)\tau+s_{2})=\int_{0}^{1}\text{d}s_{1}\,\mathcal{I}(u-a\tau)\ ,

(F.11)

where we used eq. (
F.1
) in the third equality. Since a similar derivation holds for
s_{2}
in place of
s_{1}
and
b
in place of
a
, we obtain eq. (
F.10
).
